Before all the condo stuff started, I might have worried about normal things here and there, but it wasn't anything like this. It definitely wasn't taking over my life or keeping me up at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty concentrating because of your worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It happens a lot at work, actually. I'll be right in the middle of writing a script or helping someone out—whether it's in English or Spanish—and suddenly I realize I'm just staring at the screen. My mind just completely drifts off to everything that could go wrong, and I just lose the thread of what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Does the amount of worry feel much greater than would be reasonable given the foreclosure situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does. I mean, the foreclosure is stressful, but my worry doesn't even stay on the condo anymore. It just... it jumps around. I'll start worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I'm going to make a mistake on a support call at work. And then I'm stressing over how I'll ever save money or just move forward with my life in general. It's definitely grown into a lot more than just the housing situation.\n\nDoctor: If the foreclosure issue were resolved, do you think your excessive worry would still continue across other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess I'm not really sure. It's hard to imagine what it would be like if the foreclosure was just... done. I'd like to think the worry would stop, but since it's spread to so many other things now, I just don't know if it would go away completely.\n\nDoctor: During the past six months, have you had long stretches when nothing new happened with the foreclosure but you still worried excessively about several other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm not really sure. It feels like the foreclosure is always just... there, so I don't know if I've had any long stretches where absolutely nothing new was happening with it. I mean, I know I worry about all those other things I mentioned, but I haven't really paid attention to whether it happened during a quiet period with the condo.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F43.22\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I came in because... well, I just feel wound up almost all the time. It's like my body is constantly bracing for the next bad thing to happen. Even when I'm just sitting at my desk at work, or trying to relax and enjoy dance night, I have this tension that I can't seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Did this tension begin after a specific stressful event or change in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it did. It's been going on for about nine months now. That was right around the time my condo loan started moving toward foreclosure. So, that's really when all this started.\n\nDoctor: Besides the foreclosure situation, do you find yourself worrying excessively about other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does. The worry doesn't just stay focused on the condo anymore. It kind of... jumps around to everything. Like, I'll start stressing about whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I'm going to make a mistake on a support call at work. And then I just worry about how I'll ever save money or actually move forward with my life. It just feels like it's always something.\n\nDoctor: Does your anxiety continue even during times when you are not thinking about the foreclosure?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely does. It's like I just can't stop it once it starts. Even when I tell myself I've already checked a spreadsheet or sent an email for work, the thoughts just keep looping. One 'what if' turns into five more before I can really pull myself back from it.\n\nDoctor: Is the foreclosure situation still ongoing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it is. It's still dragging on. It hasn't been completely resolved yet, so it's just kind of hanging over my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having difficulty sleeping because of this anxiety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most nights I get into bed completely exhausted, but my brain just won't shut off. It keeps replaying all the loan notices, work tickets, or just things I might have missed that day. I usually end up lying there until 2 or 3 in the morning just thinking about it all. And even when I finally do get to sleep, I wake up feeling like I barely rested.\n\nDoctor: Over the past six months, has this excessive worry occurred on more days than not?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely more days than not. Actually, it's been going on for about nine months now. It really started happening pretty much every day right around the time the condo loan started moving toward foreclosure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually irritable since the anxiety began?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed feeling more irritable than usual, honestly. It's mostly just the worrying and the trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Before the foreclosure problems began, did you already have excessive worry about several different areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Before all the condo stuff started, I might have worried about normal things here and there, but it wasn't anything like this. It definitely wasn't taking over my life or keeping me up at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty concentrating because of your worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It happens a lot at work, actually. I'll be right in the middle of writing a script or helping someone out—whether it's in English or Spanish—and suddenly I realize I'm just staring at the screen. My mind just completely drifts off to everything that could go wrong, and I just lose the thread of what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Does the amount of worry feel much greater than would be reasonable given the foreclosure situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does. I mean, the foreclosure is stressful, but my worry doesn't even stay on the condo anymore. It just... it jumps around. I'll start worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I'm going to make a mistake on a support call at work. And then I'm stressing over how I'll ever save money or just move forward with my life in general. It's definitely grown into a lot more than just the housing situation.\n\nDoctor: If the foreclosure issue were resolved, do you think your excessive worry would still continue across other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess I'm not really sure. It's hard to imagine what it would be like if the foreclosure was just... done. I'd like to think the worry would stop, but since it's spread to so many other things now, I just don't know if it would go away completely.\n\nDoctor: During the past six months, have you had long stretches when nothing new happened with the foreclosure but you still worried excessively about several other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm not really sure. It feels like the foreclosure is always just... there, so I don't know if I've had any long stretches where absolutely nothing new was happening with it. I mean, I know I worry about all those other things I mentioned, but I haven't really paid attention to whether it happened during a quiet period with the condo.\n\nDoctor: When there are no new developments with the foreclosure, does your worry about other areas of life continue at about the same level?\n\nPatient: I... I really couldn't say for sure. Like I said, the foreclosure is just kind of a constant background noise for me right now. So even if there isn't a new letter or a phone call on a specific day, the stress of it is still hanging over me. I haven't really noticed if my worrying about work or my family gets better or worse depending on that. It all just sort of blends together at this point.\n\nDoctor: When you worry about work, family, or money, does that worry usually start because you are thinking about the foreclosure, or does it arise independently of it?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure about that either. Sometimes I think it starts with the foreclosure and just spirals out from there. But other times, I'll just be sitting at work and suddenly start worrying about whether I'm doing a good job, or I'll start stressing about my mom's health, and it doesn't really feel connected to the condo at all. So I guess it could be both? It's just hard to untangle it all in my head.\n\nDoctor: On a typical day, does your anxiety continue even during periods when you are not thinking about the foreclosure at all?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely does. Even if I'm not actively thinking about the condo, the worry doesn't just stop. It just... shifts to something else. It doesn't stay on the condo anymore; it jumps around. Like, I'll start worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me over something small, or if I'm going to make a mistake on a support call at work. Or I'll just get stuck thinking about how I'm ever going to save money or just... move forward with my life. The anxiety is still there, it just finds a different target.\n\nDoctor: During the past nine months, have you had any full days when you were not thinking about the foreclosure but still experienced the same level of excessive worry about other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, it's been going on for so long now, I can't really remember a single full day where the foreclosure didn't cross my mind at least a little bit. It's just always there somewhere.\n\nDoctor: If the foreclosure were completely resolved tomorrow, do you believe your excessive worry about work, family, money, and other areas would continue at about the same level?\n\nPatient: I... I honestly don't know. It's hard to say. I'd like to think that if the condo situation was just suddenly fixed tomorrow, a lot of the stress would go away. But since I already worry about work and my family so much anyway... I'm really not sure if it would actually stop, or if I'd just keep worrying about everything else just as much. I really can't say for sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Actually, it's been going on for about nine months now. It really started happening pretty much every day right around the time the condo loan started moving toward foreclosure.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually irritable since the anxiety began?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed feeling more irritable than usual, honestly. It's mostly just the worrying and the trouble sleeping.\n\nDoctor: Before the foreclosure problems began, did you already have excessive worry about several different areas of your life?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Before all the condo stuff started, I might have worried about normal things here and there, but it wasn't anything like this. It definitely wasn't taking over my life or keeping me up at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty concentrating because of your worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It happens a lot at work, actually. I'll be right in the middle of writing a script or helping someone out—whether it's in English or Spanish—and suddenly I realize I'm just staring at the screen. My mind just completely drifts off to everything that could go wrong, and I just lose the thread of what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Does the amount of worry feel much greater than would be reasonable given the foreclosure situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does. I mean, the foreclosure is stressful, but my worry doesn't even stay on the condo anymore. It just... it jumps around. I'll start worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I'm going to make a mistake on a support call at work. And then I'm stressing over how I'll ever save money or just move forward with my life in general. It's definitely grown into a lot more than just the housing situation.\n\nDoctor: If the foreclosure issue were resolved, do you think your excessive worry would still continue across other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess I'm not really sure. It's hard to imagine what it would be like if the foreclosure was just... done. I'd like to think the worry would stop, but since it's spread to so many other things now, I just don't know if it would go away completely.\n\nDoctor: During the past six months, have you had long stretches when nothing new happened with the foreclosure but you still worried excessively about several other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm not really sure. It feels like the foreclosure is always just... there, so I don't know if I've had any long stretches where absolutely nothing new was happening with it. I mean, I know I worry about all those other things I mentioned, but I haven't really paid attention to whether it happened during a quiet period with the condo.\n\nDoctor: When there are no new developments with the foreclosure, does your worry about other areas of life continue at about the same level?\n\nPatient: I... I really couldn't say for sure. Like I said, the foreclosure is just kind of a constant background noise for me right now. So even if there isn't a new letter or a phone call on a specific day, the stress of it is still hanging over me. I haven't really noticed if my worrying about work or my family gets better or worse depending on that. It all just sort of blends together at this point.\n\nDoctor: When you worry about work, family, or money, does that worry usually start because you are thinking about the foreclosure, or does it arise independently of it?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure about that either. Sometimes I think it starts with the foreclosure and just spirals out from there. But other times, I'll just be sitting at work and suddenly start worrying about whether I'm doing a good job, or I'll start stressing about my mom's health, and it doesn't really feel connected to the condo at all. So I guess it could be both? It's just hard to untangle it all in my head.\n\nDoctor: On a typical day, does your anxiety continue even during periods when you are not thinking about the foreclosure at all?\n\nPatient: Yes, it definitely does. Even if I'm not actively thinking about the condo, the worry doesn't just stop. It just... shifts to something else. It doesn't stay on the condo anymore; it jumps around. Like, I'll start worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me over something small, or if I'm going to make a mistake on a support call at work. Or I'll just get stuck thinking about how I'm ever going to save money or just... move forward with my life. The anxiety is still there, it just finds a different target.\n\nDoctor: During the past nine months, have you had any full days when you were not thinking about the foreclosure but still experienced the same level of excessive worry about other areas of your life?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, it's been going on for so long now, I can't really remember a single full day where the foreclosure didn't cross my mind at least a little bit. It's just always there somewhere.\n\nDoctor: If the foreclosure were completely resolved tomorrow, do you believe your excessive worry about work, family, money, and other areas would continue at about the same level?\n\nPatient: I... I honestly don't know. It's hard to say. I'd like to think that if the condo situation was just suddenly fixed tomorrow, a lot of the stress would go away. But since I already worry about work and my family so much anyway... I'm really not sure if it would actually stop, or if I'd just keep worrying about everything else just as much. I really can't say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Most of the time, is your worry mainly about the foreclosure and its consequences, or is it mainly about unrelated areas of life?\n\nPatient: I think it's mostly about the unrelated areas now. Like I mentioned a minute ago, my worry really doesn't stay on the condo anymore. It just jumps around. I'll spend most of my time stressing over whether my cousin is upset with me, or if I'm going to mess up on a support call at work, or just... how I'm ever going to save money and move forward with my life. So even though the foreclosure is always kind of in the background, the bulk of my worry is definitely jumping between all those other things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I really couldn't say for sure. Like I said, the foreclosure is just kind of a constant background noise for me right now.
